Major UK Property Portals**\n\nRightmove and Zoopla dominate eyeballs for UK home search – any portal advertising strategy will revolve around them, with OTM as a growing alternative. Their ad offerings span display, sponsored listings, and email, with costs generally *high (often custom-quoted) in exchange for high-intent exposure*\n\n* Rightmove\n\n   **Rightmove:** Rightmove is the UK’s largest property portal, with around *40 million unique visitors and 1.4 billion page views per month*\n\n   [rightmove.co.uk](https://www.rightmove.co.uk/c/display-advertising/#:~:text=Our%20home%20moving%20audience%20is,we%20see%20an%20average%20of%E2%80%A6)\n\n   . Advertising on Rightmove gives access to this enormous in-market audience of homebuyers. Available formats include traditional\n\n   **display banner ads**\n\n   on the site, as well as newer\n\n   **“native” ads**\n\n   embedded in search results. For example, Rightmove’s\n\n   *Native Search Ads*\n\n   appear within property listing pages and can even include video or rich content, blending in with the user’s search experience\n\n   [hub.rightmove.co.uk](https://hub.rightmove.co.uk/native-search-ad/#:~:text=Make%20your%20direct%20response%20marketing,work%20harder)\n\n   . This native approach yields higher engagement (Rightmove cites a \\+63% boost in engagement for native formats)\n\n   [hub.rightmove.co.uk](https://hub.rightmove.co.uk/native-search-ad/#:~:text=seamless%20experience)\n\n   while targeting users “in the mindset” of moving. Rightmove also allows\n\n   **retargeting**\n\n   – using third-party tools to reach its visitors even after they leave the site\n\n   [rightmove.co.uk](https://www.rightmove.co.uk/c/display-advertising/#:~:text=Rightmove%E2%80%99s%20audience%20covers%20a%20broad,after%20they%20leave%20our%20website)\n\n   . One advantage Rightmove stresses is the\n\n   *scarcity of ads*\n\n   on its platform – any given ad faces little competition on the page, so it can capture user attention more easily\n\n   [rightmove.co.uk](https://www.rightmove.co.uk/c/display-advertising/#:~:text=We%20can%20help%20you%20really,stand%20out)\n\n   .\n\n  * **Reach:** \\~40M unique monthly visitors (about 140M visits) on Rightmove, making it *“the UK’s biggest home moving audience.”* This is roughly double the traffic of its nearest competitor.\n\n     [rightmove.co.uk](https://www.rightmove.co.uk/c/display-advertising/#:~:text=Our%20home%20moving%20audience%20is,we%20see%20an%20average%20of%E2%80%A6)\n\n  * **Ad formats:** Display banners (various sizes/positions), native in-search ads, sponsored content in high-traffic sections, and possibly email newsletter placements or partner emails (Rightmove invites advertisers to discuss custom opportunities). For estate agents and developers, Rightmove also offers premium listing options (e.g. Featured Property or Premium Listing to make a property stand out in search results), but those are geared toward boosting individual property listings rather than third-party brand ads.\n\n     [rightmove.co.uk](https://www.rightmove.co.uk/c/display-advertising/#:~:text=get%20in%20touch,talk%20through%20opportunities%20with%20you)\n\n  * **Costs:** Rightmove does not publish a rate card publicly – pricing is typically on a bespoke basis. It’s well-known that advertising on Rightmove is **premium-priced** due to its dominance. Estate agents pay substantial subscription fees just to list properties: in 2021 the *average revenue per agent advertiser was £1,189 per month* (this includes basic listing packages). A *basic agency package was reported around £720 per month per branch* for sales listings. For display advertising, costs are typically quoted via Rightmove’s sales team. Large banner campaigns likely run into the tens of thousands of pounds for campaigns that span multiple weeks on high-traffic pages. In short, expect a **high CPM** relative to other channels – the trade-off is a very targeted audience actively looking at property. Rightmove’s value proposition is that although *“not cheap by any stretch”*, its effectiveness and massive reach often justify the cost for those who need to be in front of active home-seekers.\n\n     [yopa.co.uk](https://www.yopa.co.uk/homeowners-hub/advertising-your-home-on-rightmove/#:~:text=Rightmove%20offers%20a%20range%20of,with%20customer%20numbers%20hitting%2018%2C969)\n\n     [propertyinvestmentproject.co.uk](https://www.propertyinvestmentproject.co.uk/blog/fight-rightmove-zoopla-vs-onthemarket/#:~:text=Rightmove%20%2F%20Zoopla%20Vs%20OnTheMarket,branch%20of%20a%20lettings%20agency)\n\n     [rightmove.co.uk](https://www.rightmove.co.uk/c/display-advertising/#:~:text=get%20in%20touch,talk%20through%20opportunities%20with%20you)\n\n     [yopa.co.uk](https://www.yopa.co.uk/homeowners-hub/advertising-your-home-on-rightmove/#:~:text=Although%20not%20cheap%20by%20any,different%20properties%20throughout%20the%20year)\n\n* Zoopla\n\n   **Zoopla:** Zoopla is the second-largest UK property portal, with roughly *45 million visits a month*\n\n   [surewise.com](https://www.surewise.com/storage-insurance/articles/advertise-zoopla-rightmove-and-primelocation/#:~:text=There%20are%20a%20few%20property,in%20the%20online%20property%20market)\n\n   (about half of Rightmove’s traffic). Zoopla, along with its sister sites\n\n   **PrimeLocation**\n\n   and others in its network, offers a range of advertising options for brands and property professionals. Through the\n\n   **Zoopla Advantage**\n\n   marketing platform, advertisers can access:\n\n   **on-site display ads**\n\n   ,\n\n   **sponsored listings**\n\n   , and unique sponsorship opportunities. For example, Zoopla offers\n\n   **Property Alert Sponsorship**\n\n   , which lets a brand sponsor the email alerts sent to users when new properties hit the market\n\n   [advantage.zoopla.co.uk](https://advantage.zoopla.co.uk/advertise-on-zoopla/#:~:text=,Valuation%20Booster)\n\n   . These property alert emails are extremely high-engagement touchpoints with active buyers. Zoopla also sells\n\n   **Featured Agent**\n\n   slots (prominent branding for an agent or advertiser in a given area’s search results),\n\n   **In-Search Ads**\n\n   (native ads within the property listings list, similar to Rightmove’s native ads)\n\n   [advantage.zoopla.co.uk](https://advantage.zoopla.co.uk/advertise-on-zoopla/#:~:text=,Valuation%20Booster)\n\n   ,\n\n   **Area Sponsorships**\n\n   (exclusive branding tied to searches in a particular town/area), and\n\n   **Property Sponsorships**\n\n   (sponsor a specific development or set of listings). In addition, Zoopla offers\n\n   **Remarketing solutions**\n\n   to target its audience off-site using its first-party data, and dedicated\n\n   **Email campaigns**\n\n   to reach Zoopla’s user base via bespoke email blasts\n\n   [advantage.zoopla.co.uk](https://advantage.zoopla.co.uk/advertise-on-zoopla/#:~:text=,Email%20Campaigns)\n\n   [advantage.zoopla.co.uk](https://advantage.zoopla.co.uk/advertise-on-zoopla/#:~:text=%2A%20%20On,Email%20Campaigns)\n\n   .\n\n  * **Reach:** \\~45M monthly visits on Zoopla’s site, plus additional reach through its extended network (PrimeLocation, local news site partnerships, etc.) and a large registered user base receiving email alerts. While smaller than Rightmove, this is still a **huge audience of home movers**. Zoopla often highlights use of its *“millions of visitors”* and rich first-party data to precisely target segments (buyers, renters, investors, etc.).\n\n     [surewise.com](https://www.surewise.com/storage-insurance/articles/advertise-zoopla-rightmove-and-primelocation/#:~:text=There%20are%20a%20few%20property,in%20the%20online%20property%20market)\n\n     [advantage.zoopla.co.uk](https://advantage.zoopla.co.uk/advertise-on-zoopla/#:~:text=Advertise%20on%20Zoopla%20Get%20results,party%20data)\n\n  * **Ad formats:** On-site display (banners, MPU ads), native in-listing ads, sponsored positions (Featured Agent, Premium Listings with bigger images or priority placement), email newsletter and alert sponsorship, content partnerships (advertorials on their blog or research hub), and retargeting ads powered by Zoopla’s data. Zoopla also provides tools like a **Valuation Tool widget** – sponsoring or integrating that can be a way to capture leads (e.g. an advertiser could sponsor the “What’s my house worth?” tool on Zoopla).\n\n  * **Costs:** Like Rightmove, Zoopla’s advertising costs are typically arranged case-by-case. As a benchmark, online estate agents that list on Zoopla as part of packages often charge around **£50** to sellers for inclusion on Zoopla (via intermediaries), but for **brand advertising**, costs will be higher. Zoopla historically has been slightly more budget-friendly for agents than Rightmove – some agents report legacy rates “barely increased… perhaps they value loyalty” – but for new advertisers, prices have risen as Zoopla grew. Expect CPMs in the high single digits (£5–£15) and sponsorship packages in the thousands. For example, sponsoring email alerts or a specific area for a month might run a few thousand pounds depending on the region and subscriber count (exact figures would come from Zoopla’s sales team). The key benefit is leveraging Zoopla’s **first-party data** to target by intent (e.g. showing your ad only to users searching in a certain price bracket or location). Zoopla explicitly promotes that advertisers can *“put your brand in front of millions of visitors using our first-party data.”*.\n\n     [blog.openrent.co.uk](https://blog.openrent.co.uk/advertise-on-rightmove-and-zoopla/#:~:text=OpenRent%20blog,That%20is)\n\n     [propertyinvestmentproject.co.uk](https://www.propertyinvestmentproject.co.uk/blog/fight-rightmove-zoopla-vs-onthemarket/#:~:text=30,Perhaps%20they%20value%20loyalty)\n\n     [advantage.zoopla.co.uk](https://advantage.zoopla.co.uk/advertise-on-zoopla/#:~:text=Advertise%20on%20Zoopla%20Get%20results,party%20data)\n\n* On The Market\n\n   **OnTheMarket:** OnTheMarket (OTM) is the third major property portal in the UK. Its audience size has historically been smaller, but it’s growing rapidly after a recent acquisition by CoStar Group. As of early 2024, OTM reported an *81% year-on-year increase in unique monthly visitors*\n\n   [onthemarket.com](https://www.onthemarket.com/content/onthemarket-leads-and-traffic-surge-following-costar-group-acquisition/#:~:text=Highlights%20include%3A)\n\n   , positioning itself as\n\n   *“the fastest growing portal”*\n\n   . While it still trails Rightmove and Zoopla in traffic, OTM now has over 14,000 estate agents listing (and tens of thousands of properties)\n\n   [onthemarket.com](https://www.onthemarket.com/content/onthemarket-leads-and-traffic-surge-following-costar-group-acquisition/#:~:text=Our%20agent%20office%20count%20has,hundreds%20of%20millions%20of%20pounds)\n\n   . For advertisers, OTM’s offerings may include\n\n   **banner advertising**\n\n   on their site and some co-branded content opportunities. OTM also launched products for agents like social media advertising packages (they will create targeted Facebook/Instagram ads for an agent’s listings)\n\n   [expert.onthemarket.com](https://expert.onthemarket.com/boost-your-listings/#:~:text=We%27ll%20create%20targeted%20Facebook%20and,can%20choose%20between%20three)\n\n   – indicating a focus on multi-channel marketing. They offer a\n\n   **“Local Search” sponsorship**\n\n   where an agent or developer can advertise a specific property or development at the top of search results in chosen areas\n\n   [expert.onthemarket.com](https://expert.onthemarket.com/brand-exposure/#:~:text=Generate%20brand%20exposure%20,search%20results%20of%20your%20choice)\n\n   .\n\n  * **Reach:** OTM doesn’t publicly quote monthly visitor numbers in absolute terms, but industry analysis suggests it’s a fraction of Zoopla’s. (One competitor claimed OTM’s traffic surge came largely from paid sources.) With CoStar’s investment, OTM is spending heavily on marketing (£46.5M in the first year post-acquisition), so traffic is climbing. If Zoopla has \\~20M+ monthly uniques, OTM might be in the single-digit millions of visits per month range as of 2024\\.\n\n     [onlinemarketplaces.com](https://www.onlinemarketplaces.com/articles/zoopla-questions-onthemarket-traffic-claims-as-rivalry-begins-to-heat-up/#:~:text=Zoopla%20,the%20bounce)\n\n     [onthemarket.com](https://www.onthemarket.com/content/onthemarket-leads-and-traffic-surge-following-costar-group-acquisition/#:~:text=with%20over%2014%2C000%20advertisers%20now,hundreds%20of%20millions%20of%20pounds)\n\n  * **Ad formats:** Likely **display ads** on the OTM site (branding ads for related services – e.g. finance, new homes – on their search pages). **Featured property/development ads** that pin a chosen listing to the top of results as an ad. Possibly **email advertising** – OTM users can get property alerts similar to Zoopla’s, which could be sponsored. Also, **social media extension** – OTM’s “Expert” services indicate they help advertisers reach audiences on Facebook/Instagram using OTM property feeds.\n\n     [expert.onthemarket.com](https://expert.onthemarket.com/boost-your-listings/#:~:text=We%27ll%20create%20targeted%20Facebook%20and,can%20choose%20between%20three)\n\n  * **Costs:** OnTheMarket historically positioned itself as a more cost-effective portal for estate agents (even experimenting with free introductory listings). For advertising, this could translate to more **competitive pricing** than Rightmove/Zoopla, at least while OTM builds its audience. Anecdotal data from agents suggests OTM’s cost per lead can be higher initially (due to lower traffic), but the new investment aims to change that. We can expect OTM to offer **bundled deals** or incentives. For example, an agent might pay a fixed monthly fee that includes premium placement on OTM plus some retargeting ads on social media. For a startup like HomeTruth, if budgets are tight, OTM could be worth exploring for niche exposure, but the scale is smaller.\n\n     [propertyinvestmentproject.co.uk](https://www.propertyinvestmentproject.co.uk/blog/fight-rightmove-zoopla-vs-onthemarket/#:~:text=andrewa%20%2015th%20February%2C%202015,01%3A49)\n\n     [propertyinvestmentproject.co.uk](https://www.propertyinvestmentproject.co.uk/blog/fight-rightmove-zoopla-vs-onthemarket/#:~:text=zero%20traffic%20in%20comparison%20and,more%20on%20OTM%20right%20now)\n\n* Other Property Portals\n\n   Beyond the “big three,” the UK has niche and regional portals. **Gumtree** and **Facebook Marketplace** host property listings (mainly rentals or FSBO sales) where one could post content relatively cheaply, but these aren’t traditional display advertising platforms. **PrimeLocation** is part of Zoopla’s network (targeting higher-end properties) and advertising there is usually bundled with Zoopla packages. **LoopNet/Costar** focus on commercial property. For residential, the vast majority of online homebuyer traffic is on Rightmove, Zoopla, and OTM. There was also a brief emergence of **Houser** (which tried to aggregate listings without agent permission)\n\n   [propertyinvestmentproject.co.uk](https://www.propertyinvestmentproject.co.uk/blog/fight-rightmove-zoopla-vs-onthemarket/#:~:text=It%20is%20exactly%20the%20approach,all%20our%20properties%20onto%20%27Houser)\n\n---\n\n# **Meta (Facebook/Instagram) Advertising Costs for UK Homebuyer Audience**\n\n* Advertising on Meta (Facebook and Instagram) is a key channel to reach UK homebuyers, landlords, and second-home owners at scale.\n\n   Advertising on Meta (Facebook and Instagram) is a key channel to reach UK homebuyers, landlords, and second-home owners at scale. **Facebook** alone has an estimated 55–56 million active users in the UK\n\n   [rexsoftware.com](https://www.rexsoftware.com/uk/articles/facebook-ad-benchmarks-for-properties-in-uk#:~:text=Facebook%20is%20still%20one%20of,people%20at%20the%20right%20time)\n\n   , and Instagram tens of millions more, covering a broad swath of the adult population. The advantage is the ability to\n\n   **micro-target**\n\n   this audience by demographics, interests, and behavior:\n\n  * You can target by **location** (e.g. people in the UK or even specific cities or postal codes if focusing on certain markets).  \n  * Target by **interests/behaviors** such as likely to move, interested in property investment, browsing estate agent websites, or even those who have interacted with estate agency pages. Facebook’s data and lookalike audience capabilities can, for example, target “house-hunting enthusiasts” or “landlord interests” etc.  \n  * Create **custom audiences** like retargeting visitors of HomeTruth’s website or uploading a list of known leads to reach them on social.  \n  * Use **life events** targeting (Facebook has an option to target users who have recently listed their home for sale, or recently moved, etc., which can indirectly catch home-sellers/buyers).  \n* **Cost metrics on Meta:** Facebook/Instagram costs are typically measured in **CPM** (cost per thousand impressions) and **CPC** (cost per click), among other metrics. These costs can vary by campaign objective, audience competitiveness, and ad quality, but we have some benchmarks:\n\n  * **CPM (Cost per 1,000 impressions):** In the UK, average CPM rates are around *$9–$11 USD* (≈£7–£9) according to recent data. Real-world campaigns often see CPMs in the £4–£8 range for broad targeting. In fact, for the property sector, one report found an average CPM of *£4.09* for Facebook ads in the UK real estate industry, which is quite efficient. (Competitive sectors like finance can be higher; broad consumer campaigns can be lower if optimized for reach).\n\n     [enhencer.com](https://enhencer.com/blog/cpm-of-facebook-ads-2024#:~:text=Belgium%2C%20Switzerland%2C%20Germany%2C%20and%20the,Japan%2C%20the%20Korean%20Republic%2C%20and)\n\n     [rexsoftware.com](https://www.rexsoftware.com/uk/articles/facebook-ad-benchmarks-for-properties-in-uk#:~:text=Cost%20per%20thousand%20impressions%20)\n\n  * **CPC (Cost per click):** Across all industries, Facebook traffic ads average around $0.77 per click (\\~£0.60). The **real estate category** tends to do even better – globally averaging about *$0.65 per click* (≈£0.50) with a CTR around 2.6%. In the UK, estate agents have seen extremely low CPCs – Rex Software reports an average *£0.04 per click* for property ads, which likely reflects highly engaging listing content and local targeting. While £0.04 is unusually low, it suggests that well-targeted home ads (especially carousel ads of attractive properties) can generate clicks very cheaply. More typical might be £0.20–£0.50 per click for a general homebuyer-targeted ad with informative content.\n\n     [wordstream.com](https://www.wordstream.com/blog/facebook-ads-benchmarks-2024#:~:text=,83)\n\n     [wordstream.com](https://www.wordstream.com/blog/facebook-ads-benchmarks-2024#:~:text=Home%20%26%20Home%20Improvement%20%240,Travel%20%240.42%202.20)\n\n     [rexsoftware.com](https://www.rexsoftware.com/uk/articles/facebook-ad-benchmarks-for-properties-in-uk#:~:text=Cost%20per%20click%20)\n\n  * **CTR (Click-through rate):** This measures ad engagement. For real estate ads, CTRs can be above average. The UK property ads averaged a strong *10.7% CTR* on Facebook (likely for ads that look like property listings, which naturally draw clicks from curious buyers). Let’s temper that: if HomeTruth is advertising a service (not a specific house for sale), CTRs might be more in line with typical ads (1-3%). But compelling offers (e.g. “Find out your home’s true worth – click here”) could drive CTRs in the several percent range, which keeps CPC low.\n\n     [rexsoftware.com](https://www.rexsoftware.com/uk/articles/facebook-ad-benchmarks-for-properties-in-uk#:~:text=Click)\n\n* **Budget considerations:** Meta ads are highly scalable – you can start with a small daily budget and ramp up as you see results. For example:\n\n  * A **test budget** of say **£500** might buy roughly *100,000–125,000 impressions* (assuming \\~£4-£5 CPM for a broad campaign). If your ad is decent, this could result in on the order of *1,000–5,000 clicks* (depending on CTR variability – at 1% CTR it’s \\~1k clicks; at 4% CTR, 4k clicks). Those clicks could be to a landing page or app store, where conversion to sign-ups would then determine cost-per-lead.\n\n  * Scaling up, a **£5,000 budget** could deliver \\~1–1.5 million impressions. With optimization, Facebook will deliver ads to the users most likely to act, so you might maintain efficient CPC as you scale, until you saturate the core audience. At that level, one could expect tens of thousands of clicks and a significant influx of leads if the funnel is optimized.\n\n  * **Cost per lead/conversion:** This depends on the conversion rate on your site/app. But we do have a benchmark from Facebook lead ads in real estate: *\\~£65 per lead for a seller lead* using on-Facebook lead forms. That was for vendor (home seller) leads, which are quite valuable (a £65 cost to potentially win a listing worth thousands in commission is cheap). For HomeTruth, if the conversion goal is a sign-up or an inquiry, the cost could be much lower. Many B2C apps aim for cost per app install or sign-up in the £5–£20 range via Facebook. With a well-targeted campaign, it’s feasible that *for £50 you acquire multiple leads/users on Meta*, whereas that might be the cost of a single click or lead via some other channels.\n\n     [rexsoftware.com](https://www.rexsoftware.com/uk/articles/facebook-ad-benchmarks-for-properties-in-uk#:~:text=Cost%20per%20lead%20)\n\n* It’s important to note that Facebook costs can fluctuate:\n\n  * More competitive audiences (e.g., if you target wealthy landlords with high incomes, you may overlap with many financial services advertisers and see higher CPMs).  \n  * Seasonal factors (housing market spikes in spring might attract more advertising, driving up costs slightly).  \n  * Ad quality relevance: a highly relevant ad (good engagement, positive feedback) will be rewarded by Facebook with lower effective costs, whereas an ad that people hide or ignore will cost more to deliver.  \n* **Instagram** as part of Meta often has similar CPMs, sometimes slightly cheaper or more expensive depending on format (Story ads vs Feed ads). But in general, you can consider Instagram part of the same buy (Meta allows placements on both).\n\n   In terms of **ad formats** on Meta for this space:\n\n  * **Image/carousel ads:** e.g., a carousel showing a few properties or features of HomeTruth, with a call-to-action.\n\n  * **Video ads:** demos of the product, client testimonials – video can improve engagement for complex offerings.\n\n  * **Lead ads:** where users can submit their email/interest in-app without leaving Facebook. These can yield higher volume of leads (like the £65 seller lead example), but lead quality needs nurturing.\n\n     [rexsoftware.com](https://www.rexsoftware.com/uk/articles/facebook-ad-benchmarks-for-properties-in-uk#:~:text=Cost%20per%20lead%20)\n\n  * **Sponsored posts/branded content:** perhaps working with influencers in property space on Instagram (somewhat different approach, but worth noting as a Meta sub-channel).\n\n* Overall, **Meta’s advantage is cost-effective, broad reach** and sophisticated targeting. You can reach people *before* they even arrive at portals like Rightmove – for instance, targeting aspirational first-time buyers who are casually scrolling social media and pulling them into your funnel. The cost per thousand impressions on Meta (single-digit pounds) is dramatically lower than, say, a property portal homepage takeover might be.\n\n---\n\n# **High-Impact Acquisition Strategies Beyond Paid Ads**\n\n* Relying solely on paid ads (social or portals) can be expensive in the long run. Successful PropTech startups often use creative growth tactics to acquire traffic and users at lower cost. Here are key strategies and examples:\n\n   **Organic Growth & Content Marketing**\n\n   Building **organic traffic** via valuable content and SEO is a cost-efficient strategy. Many PropTech companies establish themselves as authoritative sources of property information, which draws in their target audience naturally. For example, **Zoopla** boosted its traffic in its early days by offering free **house price data, valuation tools, and local market insights**, making itself a destination for homeowners curious about their home’s value. Regular content like **house price indexes and market reports** (which Zoopla still publishes monthly\n\n   [advantage.zoopla.co.uk](https://advantage.zoopla.co.uk/advertise-on-zoopla/#:~:text=,September%202024)\n\n   ) not only improve SEO but also earn media coverage, funneling organic traffic.\n\n   A good content strategy focuses on **delivering value** to the target audience. This can include:\n\n  * **Blogs and Guides:** Articles on home-buying tips, rental how-tos, landlord guides, second-home tax advice, etc., to capture Google searches that your potential users are making. If HomeTruth provides a unique service (e.g. investment analysis or property transparency), publishing *“how it works” explanations and industry insights* can educate consumers while ranking on search engines.\n\n  * **Thought Leadership Pieces:** Conduct interviews with property experts or publish data-driven studies. *“Content that truly resonates with your audience and addresses their needs can establish you as an authority,”* as one PropTech marketer notes. For instance, Bright Spaces (a proptech startup) created value-driven content like expert interviews and case studies to build credibility.\n\n     [therecursive.com](https://therecursive.com/tried-and-tested-marketing-activities-for-proptech-startups/#:~:text=Craft%20Value)\n\n     [therecursive.com](https://therecursive.com/tried-and-tested-marketing-activities-for-proptech-startups/#:~:text=Craft%20Value)\n\n  * **Free Tools or Calculators:** Offer calculators (stamp duty calculator, rent vs buy calculator) or interactive tools. These not only attract users but often earn backlinks (improving SEO). Rightmove and Zoopla both host things like mortgage calculators and local area guides to keep organic visitors engaged.\n\n  * **Lead Magnets:** E-books or checklists (e.g. “Homebuyer’s Checklist” or “Guide to Being a Landlord”) that users can download in exchange for their email. This grows an email list for **email marketing** – a free/low-cost channel to nurture leads with newsletters, property news, etc.\n\n* Organic growth takes time to build, but it can yield compounding returns. **Case study:** Digital mortgage broker **Habito** grew to 50,000 customers in 18 months largely by differentiating their brand and content in a “dull” mortgage market\n\n   [thechallengerproject.com](https://thechallengerproject.com/blog/2022/habito-cmo-abba-newbery-interview#:~:text=Mortgages%20are%20dull,as%20CMO%20to%20provide%20the)\n\n   . They invested in making the topic engaging (e.g. their edgy “Hell or Habito” campaign and educational content), which drove a lot of word-of-mouth and direct traffic. While Habito did use paid ads too, their CMO noted that creating a\n\n   *famous brand*\n\n   and content was key in a category where people rely on trust\n\n   [thechallengerproject.com](https://thechallengerproject.com/blog/2022/habito-cmo-abba-newbery-interview#:~:text=transparency%20than%20the%20banks%20or,a%20match%20made%20in%20heaven)\n\n   [thechallengerproject.com](https://thechallengerproject.com/blog/2022/habito-cmo-abba-newbery-interview#:~:text=You%20once%20said%20that%20it%E2%80%99s,so%20important%20as%20an%20objective)\n\n   . The lesson:\n\n   **SEO \\+ strong content marketing \\= steady inbound traffic**\n\n   that doesn’t require paying per click.\n\n   **Referral Programs & Viral Loops**\n\n   Encouraging your existing users to **refer others** can accelerate growth with minimal marketing spend. Many iconic startups (though not all in PropTech) have leveraged referral incentives to great effect. **Airbnb** is a classic example: it implemented a referral program where users got travel credit for inviting friends. This program ultimately produced *300% more bookings and signups* after its revamp\n\n   [cloudsponge.com](https://www.cloudsponge.com/blog/airbnb-referral-program/#:~:text=Overall%2C%20referrals%20increased%20bookings%20by,bookings%20and%20signups%20than%20before)\n\n   , and referrals came to account for a significant share of Airbnb’s user acquisition. While HomeTruth’s target (homebuyers, landlords) is a different domain, a referral scheme can still work if there’s a valuable incentive.\n\n   For instance, HomeTruth could offer **£X credit, gift cards, or premium features unlocked** when a user refers a friend who signs up or completes a certain action. **Dropbox** famously grew 3900% in 15 months via referral credits for cloud storage; in real estate, the incentive might be different (perhaps entry into a prize draw for new home gadgets, or a discount on HomeTruth’s services if applicable). The key is to make each invite feel like a *“gift, not a promotion,”* as Airbnb learned\n\n   [cloudsponge.com](https://www.cloudsponge.com/blog/airbnb-referral-program/#:~:text=%3E%20,a%20gift%2C%20not%20a%20promotion)\n\n   – i.e. emphasize how the referred friend benefits.\n\n   Another aspect is **word-of-mouth loops** without formal rewards. If HomeTruth delivers a great experience, encourage users to share testimonials or post on social media. Building a **community or ambassador program** can amplify this. Satisfied customers (first-time buyers you helped, successful landlords using the platform) can become evangelists. *“Your clients and partners can be your most potent allies in amplifying your brand,”* notes one PropTech marketing lead, who suggests nurturing relationships with early users and encouraging testimonials and social sharing\n\n   [therecursive.com](https://therecursive.com/tried-and-tested-marketing-activities-for-proptech-startups/#:~:text=Harness%20the%20Power%20of%20Ambassadors)\n\n   . In real estate,\n\n   **trust**\n\n   is crucial – people often rely on recommendations from friends (84% trust recommendations per Nielsen\n\n   [cloudsponge.com](https://www.cloudsponge.com/blog/airbnb-referral-program/#:~:text=Taru%20Bhargava%2C%20author%20at%20AppVirality%2C,referral%20experience%20is%20so%20effective)\n\n   ). Harnessing that via referrals and reviews can yield high-quality leads at virtually no cost.\n\n   **Strategic Partnerships**\n\n   Partnering with complementary businesses can quickly boost traffic by tapping into an existing audience. In the property sector, consider **alliances that align with the home-buying journey**:\n\n  * **Financial Services:** e.g. a partnership with a mortgage broker or bank. If HomeTruth is a property insights platform, teaming up with a popular mortgage comparison site could funnel their users to you (and vice versa) – perhaps HomeTruth provides market data to that site’s users, and in return gets promotion to their client base.  \n  * **Moving Services & Utilities:** Many portals and agencies partner with moving companies, insurance providers, or utility switching services to cross-sell. HomeTruth could strike a deal with, say, a home insurance startup or a removals firm to co-market each other. For instance, second-home owners might need special insurance; a tie-in campaign could offer a discount on insurance when they sign up for HomeTruth, leveraging the insurance company’s mailing list for promotion.  \n  * **Real Estate Agencies or Developers:** If HomeTruth’s product is something that helps homebuyers (perhaps a transparency report on properties or a decision-making tool), estate agents or new-build developers could be partners rather than competitors. An agent could offer HomeTruth’s service as a value-add to their clients (which brings you users), or HomeTruth could feature selected properties or developments in content, sponsored by those developers.  \n  * **Community and Events:** Sponsoring or partnering on events (homebuyer seminars, landlord networking events) can get your name out organically. For example, appearing at a **first-time buyer webinar** run by a popular property blogger, or co-hosting a landlord Q\\&A session with a landlord association, introduces HomeTruth to a pre-qualified audience.  \n* Strategic partnerships essentially allow you to **share audiences**. They can be highly cost-effective – often a quid-pro-quo exchange of visibility rather than a paid placement. A successful case study in PropTech is how **Purplebricks** leveraged media and even entertainment partnerships. They did product placement (their brand appeared in *Coronation Street*, a popular soap)\n\n   [theboar.org](https://theboar.org/2024/02/the-company-that-was-bought-for-a-pound/#:~:text=Their%202016%20%E2%80%98Commisery%E2%80%99%20campaign%20surrounding,one%20point%2C%20the%20stock%20was)\n\n   and collaborated with comedian Mo Gilligan on ads\n\n   [theboar.org](https://theboar.org/2024/02/the-company-that-was-bought-for-a-pound/#:~:text=Their%202016%20%E2%80%98Commisery%E2%80%99%20campaign%20surrounding,one%20point%2C%20the%20stock%20was)\n\n   . These creative partnerships embedded the brand in popular culture, boosting recognition. The result: Purplebricks achieved\n\n   **98% brand awareness in the UK**\n\n   by 2019\n\n   [theboar.org](https://theboar.org/2024/02/the-company-that-was-bought-for-a-pound/#:~:text=With%20this%20unique%20zero,as%20making%202019%E2%80%99s%20%E2%80%98Superbrands%E2%80%99%20list)\n\n   . While not all partnerships need to be that high-profile, it shows the power of thinking beyond traditional ads – find channels where your target audience already engages, and integrate your brand there.\n\n   **Public Relations (PR) and Press Coverage**\n\n   **PR campaigns** and media coverage can provide massive exposure at relatively low cost. Startups that can generate *buzz* or position themselves as newsworthy can get featured in newspapers, online news, TV segments, etc., bringing in traffic and users for free. There are a few ways to approach PR:\n\n  * **Data-driven PR:** Leverage any unique data or insights your platform has to create news stories. For example, HomeTruth could publish a quarterly *“Homebuyer Confidence Index”* or a report on “Top 10 Up-and-Coming Property Hotspots.” Journalists are often hungry for real estate data and trends. This is a tactic used by bigger portals: Rightmove and Zoopla regularly release reports on prices, buyer sentiment, rental trends, which get cited in the press (e.g., “according to Zoopla, UK house prices rose X% last month...”).\n\n  * **Human-interest PR:** Share compelling stories of users or the problem you solve. A PropTech startup solving a real pain point can pitch stories like “Meet the family who found their dream home thanks to \\[HomeTruth\\]” or a press release like “Startup \\[HomeTruth\\] uses tech to save homebuyers from costly mistakes.” If picked up by a site like *TechCrunch* or *PropertyWeek*, it not only drives traffic, it builds credibility.\n\n  * **Stunts or Campaigns:** Do something share-worthy. For instance, an online estate agent once ran a campaign about “commisery” (misery of paying commission) – Purplebricks’ cheeky ads around this went viral and *contributed to a 218% revenue growth* for them, winning an advertising effectiveness award. While that was a paid ad campaign, it had a PR angle in how it tapped into public frustration (earning Purplebricks a lot of free mention in discussions about agent fees). A PR stunt example: a PropTech company could offer to pay someone’s first mortgage payment as a contest – local news might pick that up.\n\n     [theboar.org](https://theboar.org/2024/02/the-company-that-was-bought-for-a-pound/#:~:text=Moreover%2C%20the%20firm%E2%80%99s%20tenacious%20approach,into%20the%20lives%20of%20British)\n\n  * **Awards and Recognition:** Apply for startup awards, PropTech innovation accolades, etc. A win or even a nomination can be leveraged in PR outreach (“award-winning platform HomeTruth…”).\n\n  * **Expert commentary:** Position the founders or team as experts. Get them speaking on industry podcasts, quoted in articles (reporters often seek commentary for property stories). Being featured as an expert source in a *prominent real estate publication or even national press* can dramatically raise profile. Real estate still has a lot of trade magazines and sites; getting an interview or contributing an op-ed can directly target industry stakeholders and indirectly consumers.\n\n     [therecursive.com](https://therecursive.com/tried-and-tested-marketing-activities-for-proptech-startups/#:~:text=Build%20Trust%20and%20Credibility)\n\n* The beauty of PR is the **implied endorsement** – a press article or TV segment carries more credibility than an ad, and it’s essentially free media. Many PropTech startups (like *Nested*, *Trussle*, *OpenRent*) used PR in their early stages to punch above their weight in exposure, often by contrasting themselves with industry incumbents in a newsworthy way (e.g., “Online agent sells homes for free – is this the end of high street agents?” was a narrative that got people talking about HouseSimple/Strike). A strong PR campaign can drive spikes of traffic and sign-ups at a very low customer acquisition cost.\n\n   **Case Studies of Efficient Scaling**\n\n   Looking at PropTech (and related) startups that scaled efficiently:\n\n  * **Purplebricks:** As mentioned, they invested heavily in marketing, but it was efficient in terms of outcome. Their bold campaign (“Commisery”) tapped into a viral concept and reportedly yielded *218% growth in revenue* in one year. They also claimed to sell a house every 9 minutes at their peak, capturing significant share. The lesson is that a *memorable, resonant message* (in their case, “save money on commission”) combined with multi-channel promotion (TV ads, billboards, PR buzz from the controversy it stirred) can rapidly build a user base – but note, Purplebricks did spend millions, so not cheap, but their cost per acquisition improved as they gained nationwide volume.\n\n     [theboar.org](https://theboar.org/2024/02/the-company-that-was-bought-for-a-pound/#:~:text=Moreover%2C%20the%20firm%E2%80%99s%20tenacious%20approach,into%20the%20lives%20of%20British)\n\n  * **Airbnb:** Though not a homebuying startup, Airbnb’s growth tactics are legendary and applicable. They famously seeded their early growth by automating posts to Craigslist (growth hack), but the big boost came from their **referral program** and **user-generated content**. Referrals gave them a *25% boost in bookings in some markets* and the refined referral 2.0 program led to *300% more signups*. They also ran a PR campaign around “live like a local” that got travelers intrigued. The takeaway: make it easy and rewarding for users to spread the word, and leverage your community for content/marketing.\n\n     [cloudsponge.com](https://www.cloudsponge.com/blog/airbnb-referral-program/#:~:text=Overall%2C%20referrals%20increased%20bookings%20by,bookings%20and%20signups%20than%20before)\n\n  * **OpenRent:** A UK platform for landlords, OpenRent grew rapidly by **undercutting traditional letting agent fees** (free listings, then a low fixed fee). They did minimal paid advertising; instead they grew through *forum recommendations and SEO*. Landlords on online forums would constantly mention OpenRent as a great solution – genuine word-of-mouth. By offering a dramatically cheaper service, they turned customers into evangelists. This shows the power of having a **strong value proposition** that markets itself (people *want* to tell others if you save them money or hassle).\n\n  * **Habito:** Habito’s marketing combined content with creative ads. They ran edgy online video ads (depicting the hell of mortgage stress in cartoonish ways) which got people sharing, and simultaneously published guides demystifying mortgages. In 2018, after launching their “Hell or Habito” campaign, they saw **brand awareness and customer volume jump** – within a year, spontaneous brand awareness doubled and customer volumes were *3.5x higher*. Notably, they moved away from purely direct-response ads to more **brand-building** advertising, which in turn made their *performance marketing (like PPC) more effective* because people recognized the brand and trusted it. This suggests that sometimes investing in brand creative can actually *lower* your acquisition costs in the long run by improving conversion rates at every touchpoint.\n\n     [marketingweek.com](https://www.marketingweek.com/habito-gave-its-brand-a-kickstart/#:~:text=To%20hell%20and%20back%3A%20How,a%20half%20times%2C%20and)\n\n     [thinkbox.tv](https://www.thinkbox.tv/case-studies/habito-builds-its-brand-to-drive-performance-in-an-unloved-category#:~:text=Habito%20builds%20its%20brand%20to,they%20embraced%20brand%20building)\n\n* In summary, PropTech startups that scaled fast did so with a mix of smart **digital tactics** (SEO, referrals, virality) and bold **strategic marketing** (brand campaigns, PR, partnerships). HomeTruth can draw from these examples to craft a growth plan that isn’t dependent only on buying ads, but rather creates a self-reinforcing loop of traffic (through content, press, and user advocacy). These methods often lead to a lower **CAC** (Customer Acquisition Cost) over time, which is crucial for efficient scaling.\n\n---\n\n# **Meta/Instagram vs Property Portal Ads vs Other Strategies**\n\n* When determining the most **cost-efficient and scalable** acquisition approach for HomeTruth, it’s useful to compare these channels:\n\n  * **Meta (Facebook/Instagram):** Extremely scalable and typically the **lowest cost per impression and click**. As noted, CPMs \\~£5 and CPC well under £1 are achievable. This means for the cost of reaching 1,000 people on a portal, you might reach 5–10× as many on Facebook. Meta excels for **broad outreach and top-of-funnel awareness**. It can drive a high volume of traffic and sign-ups quickly, and you can refine targeting as you learn which segments convert best. The trade-off: those impressions are *out-of-context* (people weren’t actively seeking property info at that moment), so conversion rates might be lower per click than a portal. You may need to **nurture** the leads from Facebook (via retargeting or email follow-ups) to turn them into active users. Nonetheless, for pure customer acquisition cost, Meta is often more efficient. It’s also very flexible – you can ramp spend up or down on short notice and A/B test creatives to optimize performance.\n\n     [rexsoftware.com](https://www.rexsoftware.com/uk/articles/facebook-ad-benchmarks-for-properties-in-uk#:~:text=Cost%20per%20thousand%20impressions%20)\n\n     [wordstream.com](https://www.wordstream.com/blog/facebook-ads-benchmarks-2024#:~:text=Home%20%26%20Home%20Improvement%20%240,Travel%20%240.42%202.20)\n\n  * **Property Portals (Rightmove/Zoopla/OTM):** These offer **access to the most intent-rich audience** – people actively looking at properties. The likelihood of conversion (say, clicking through and engaging with HomeTruth if it’s relevant to home search) can be higher *if* the ad resonates. For example, an ad on Rightmove saying “Not sure if a property is a good deal? HomeTruth gives you the insight – click here” could grab a serious buyer’s attention more effectively than a random Facebook user. However, the **cost is significantly higher**. You might be paying a premium sponsorship or high CPM to reach these users. If a single banner on Rightmove costs, hypothetically, £10,000 for a month of exposure, one must consider how many leads that will yield versus spending £10k on Facebook. If Rightmove’s banner gets, say, 1 million impressions in that time (just illustrative), that’s a £10 CPM – not bad superficially, but you have no ability to retarget those who saw it and didn’t act (unless using their retargeting product). On Facebook, £10k could generate 2 million+ impressions and you could retarget non-converters multiple times for further nudges. Also, portal ads may suffer **“banner blindness”** – users are laser-focused on listings and might ignore side ads. Rightmove itself claims an advantage of few ads on page, but that’s a double-edged sword: it means you get share of voice, but users don’t expect non-listing content, so they might tune out anything that’s not a house listing. In terms of **cost per lead**, unless HomeTruth’s offering is exactly what a portal user needs at that moment, the CAC could end up higher. Portal advertising is powerful for **brand awareness** among serious home movers and can lend credibility (appearing on Rightmove can make you look established), but for pure efficient conversion at scale, it’s more costly and limited in volume (there are only so many portal users and only so many ads you can buy there).\n\n     [rightmove.co.uk](https://www.rightmove.co.uk/c/display-advertising/#:~:text=We%20can%20help%20you%20really,stand%20out)\n\n  * **Alternative Organic Strategies:** These include content/SEO, referrals, partnerships, PR as discussed. These often have the **lowest monetary cost per acquisition** but require time and effort investment. Over the long term, a piece of content that ranks \\#1 on Google for “home buying tips” might bring in thousands of free visitors a month – essentially an extremely low CAC. Referral programs, once set up, piggyback on your users and can acquire new users for just the cost of the referral reward (which is often far less than paying for ads). Strategic partnerships might be *win-win with no money exchanged* (just barter of user access). The downside is control and scalability: you can’t immediately get 1 million visitors via SEO or referrals next week – it builds up over months and years. And PR can be hit or miss (not every press release will get picked up). However, these methods **compound**. A startup that invests in content and community can eventually enjoy a large **baseline of organic traffic** that reduces the need for heavy ad spend. This is the model of many modern companies: use content/SEO to drive half or more of leads, supplement with paid ads to accelerate growth or target where organic doesn’t reach.\n\n* In practice, the **most cost-efficient strategy** is usually a **blend**:\n\n  * In the **early stage**, you might use **Meta ads to jumpstart traction** because they can deliver results immediately. For example, run Facebook/Instagram campaigns to get your first few thousand users – you’ll pay for them, but you also gather data and hopefully some revenue or engagement that can be reinvested.  \n  * Meanwhile, **build your organic channels** (content, SEO, PR) so that over time you’re less reliant on paying for each click.  \n  * Use **referrals and partnerships** to add a multiplier to your growth without equivalent spend – e.g., every user brings one more user, effectively halving your paid acquisition cost per user.  \n  * Consider **portal advertising** in specific cases where the ROI justifies it – for instance, if HomeTruth needs to reach active landlords specifically, maybe a sponsorship on a landlord forum or a portal’s landlord section could yield high-value conversions. Or if you launch a new feature for home sellers, perhaps an OnTheMarket banner targeted at their “Value my home” page could directly get seller leads. These should be measured carefully (if a £5k portal campaign yields only 50 sign-ups, that’s £100 per sign-up – is that acceptable vs Facebook’s £10 per sign-up?).  \n* To put it succinctly: **Meta/Instagram offers a high volume of leads at a lower unit cost**, while **property portals offer lower volume but potentially higher intent leads at a higher cost**. **Organic and creative strategies offer the lowest cost per lead but require time to scale up**. For HomeTruth, a wise approach is to leverage the cost-efficiency of Meta to build an initial user base and brand presence (since £1 on Facebook can often go further than £1 on a portal in terms of raw reach\n\n   [rexsoftware.com](https://www.rexsoftware.com/uk/articles/facebook-ad-benchmarks-for-properties-in-uk#:~:text=Cost%20per%20thousand%20impressions%20)\n\n   ),\n\n   *while simultaneously investing in content, PR, and referrals to fuel sustainable growth*\n\n   . Portal advertising can be layered on as the budget grows or for targeted campaigns (e.g. a burst campaign on Rightmove for credibility and to capture hot leads during peak homebuying season, once you can afford it).\n\n   Ultimately, the **most scalable** growth will come from the channels that can continuously expand without proportional cost increases – that tends to be organic channels and virality. Paid social (Meta) is scalable up to a point (you can spend millions there as long as it stays profitable), and it’s highly cost-efficient at the start. Property portals are somewhat less scalable simply due to their finite inventory and higher costs – you wouldn’t rely on portal ads alone to get tens of thousands of users due to cost. Instead, you might use portal presence for **strategic advantage** (like trust and visibility) and use Meta and organic for **efficient user acquisition**.\n\n   In conclusion, **HomeTruth should prioritize a balanced mix**: use **Meta/Instagram ads for cost-effective, broad reach** and fast growth of the user base, double down on **content marketing, SEO, referrals, and PR for low-cost sustained growth**, and selectively use **property portal advertising or partnerships for high-intent user acquisition and brand credibility**. This combination will yield the most cost-efficient and scalable growth model, rather than depending on any single channel. By monitoring the performance and ROI of each channel (e.g., cost per lead on Facebook vs. cost per lead from a portal sponsorship vs. essentially £0 cost per organic lead), HomeTruth can allocate budget to the best performers and achieve scale while keeping acquisition costs in check.\n\n   **Sources:**\n\n  * Rightmove audience figures and advertising advantages\n\n study)\n\n     [cloudsponge.com](https://www.cloudsponge.com/blog/airbnb-referral-program/#:~:text=Overall%2C%20referrals%20increased%20bookings%20by,bookings%20and%20signups%20than%20before)\n\n  * Facebook advertising benchmarks (real estate CTR, CPC, CPM)\n\n
